Over the course of 3Q26, P&C insurers (-2%) and insurance brokers (-2%) 
underperformed the S&P 500 (+3%) amidst continued softening insurance cycle data 
points and at least partially renewed agentic/AI related disruption fears. That said, 
the group enters 3Q with some built-in earnings tailwinds from overall lighter 
catastrophe activity, higher interest rates, and a favorable sequential mix shift 
towards faster growing casualty products. Growth has softened to more normal 
levels for the industry, yet ROEs remain better than long-term averages. Investor 
focus is likely to be centered on 1) the magnitude and duration of softer insurance 
cycle conditions, 2) AI risks and opportunities, 3) capital deployment, 4) insight into 
macroeconomic conditions, and 5) stability of casualty reserves from recent 
accident years. By sub-sector we have a favorable bias towards brokers where we 
think in-line or slightly favorable results relative to expectations could result in solid 
stock reactions, given below prior soft market EV / Adj. EBITDA multiples. Within 
brokers, we favor Buy rated WTW/NP on margin/distribution momentum, 
respectively, and think Neutral rated BRO could be in for a more positive quarter. In 
Commercial lines, we favor WRB on predominantly casualty exposure and the 
strongest leverage to improving forward NII, while we favor PGR in Personal lines on 
solid growth, margin, and agentic AI positioning relative to peers. 
Insurance Brokers/Capital-Light: Insurance brokers face a modestly decelerating 
operating environment in 3Q with GSe nominal/real GDP growth falling 80bps/30bps 
sequentially, partially offset by a favorable seasonal mix shift away from property 
CAT, which has been experiencing severe pricing declines. We model fairly stable 
average Retail broker organic growth of 4.2% (4.1% 2Q26), driven by sequential 
improvement at brokers with greater pricing exposure (AJG, BRO), and offset by 
sequential deceleration from more fee-based brokers (AON, MRSH, WTW); albeit at 
higher average organic growth levels. We expect a continuation of strength in 
large/data center construction with monthly nonresidential & nonbuilding 
construction starts value accelerating to +40% YoY in July/August vs +26% in 1H26, 
though M&A/capital markets activity is likely lighter QoQ with M&A deal count/value 
completed -16%/-1% vs +18%/+42% in 2Q26. The group enters 4Q trading below 
prior soft market multiples at 11.3x EV/Adj. EBITDA, (which is likely driven at least 
partially by renewed AI disruption fears) leading to our view that solid results are 
likely to be rewarded. We expect the primary focus will be on understanding the 
insurance cycle trajectory, followed by updates on perceived risks and opportunities

from AI, capital deployment, and macro trends. Amongst Buy rated names, we favor NP 
on revenue momentum from an expanded and more productive agent distribution 
network, and favor WTW on maintaining its trajectory of above-peer margin expansion 
with solid organic growth. Additionally, we think Neutral rated BRO could have a solid 
report with a strong mix shift into faster growing casualty business, potentially 
underappreciated margin tailwinds from Accession deal integration, and a less 
pessimistic talent narrative. 
Commercial (re)insurers: We have a more cautious bias for this group despite tailwinds 
of lower overall CATs and improving NII, as risks to future core margin estimates remain 
from the downward commercial pricing trajectory, along with lingering concerns on 
casualty reserves and potentially sizable Middle East insured losses for those most 
exposed (incl. ACGL, AIG, RNR, AXS, PLGO). Relative to a pressured 2Q, pricing metrics 
should benefit from the QoQ shift towards casualty products and more limited property 
CAT exposure, leading to modestly improved pricing for large account focused names 
such as AIG/CB. Key topics are likely to be 1) P&C cycle trajectory, 2) capital 
deployment, 2) AI risks & opportunities, and 4) casualty reserves. Heading into the 
quarter, we see WRB as the best positioned stock in the group as we expect a strong 
ROE narrative on opportunistically increasing duration amidst higher interest rates, 
along with a continuation of steady Insurance loss ratios, combined with more stable 
pricing. 
Personal Lines: We have a less positive view on the setup for personal lines insurers into 
results than in recent memory, as we think core underwriting margin beats are starting 
to get harder to come by (TRV, HIG, ALL) as lower earned pricing increasingly eats into 
margins, following several quarters of fairly large beats. The growth environment 
remains competitive, leading to our expectation for NPW growth misses as pricing 
remains in the LSD negative territory for auto and MSD (but decelerating) territory for 
home insurance. The catastrophe loss read-through from ALL/PGR disclosures likely 
indicates that industry severe convective storm losses (and potentially non-cat weather) 
were still material in the quarter, despite the favorable lack of hurricanes. Agentic AI 
shopping concerns were top of mind following the release of META’s Muse this quarter, 
which we expect to be topic of discussion, with PGR best positioned in our view given its 
greater DTC presence (incl. advertising) and comparatively lower prices. We favor Buy 
rated PGR into results as we expect investors will need to revise earnings estimates 
higher on NII, along with our expectations for a modest core loss ratio beat and solid PIF 
growth.

Industry Views 
 
 
EPS Changes 
P&C insurer EPS adjustments for 3Q26 modestly increase on average due to both lower 
natural CAT expectations and modestly higher NII yields. We have made selective 
changes to company specific underlying margin estimates. Forward estimates include 
upward revisions to fixed net investment income yields due to material upward 
movement in the forward curve in addition to company specific underwriting 
adjustments. 
For insurance brokers, our EPS adjustments reflect modest adjustments to organic 
growth/margins based on economic data and other read-throughs for the quarter, as 
well as a positive outlook for short term interest rates, leading to stronger fiduciary 
investment income.

Reg AC 
We, Robert Cox, Jack Kendall and Victoria Gong, hereby certify that all of the views expressed in this report accurately reflect our personal views about 
the subject company or companies and its or their securities. We also certify that no part of our compensation was, is or will be, directly or indirectly, 
related to the specific recommendations or views expressed in this report. 
Unless otherwise stated, the individuals listed on the cover page of this report are analysts in Goldman Sachs’ Global Investment Research division. 
Contributing Authors: Robert Cox Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC, Jack Kendall Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC, Victoria Gong Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC. 
Unless otherwise stated, the individuals listed in the Contributing Authors disclosure of this report are analysts in Goldman Sachs’ Global Investment 
Research division. 
GS Factor Profile 
The Goldman Sachs Factor Profile provides investment context for a stock by comparing key attributes to the market (i.e. our universe of rated stocks) 
and its sector peers. The four key attributes depicted are: Growth, Financial Returns, Multiple (e.g. valuation) and Integrated (a composite of Growth, 
Financial Returns and Multiple). Growth, Financial Returns and Multiple are calculated by using normalized ranks for specific metrics for each stock. The 
normalized ranks for the metrics are then averaged and converted into percentiles for the relevant attribute. The precise calculation of each metric may 
vary depending on the fiscal year, industry and region, but the standard approach is as follows: 
Growth is based on a stock’s forward-looking sales growth, EBITDA growth and EPS growth (for financial stocks, only EPS and sales growth), with a 
higher percentile indicating a higher growth company. Financial Returns is based on a stock’s forward-looking ROE, ROCE and CROCI (for financial 
stocks, only ROE), with a higher percentile indicating a company with higher financial returns. Multiple is based on a stock’s forward-looking P/E, P/B, 
price/dividend (P/D), EV/EBITDA, EV/FCF and EV/Debt Adjusted Cash Flow (DACF) (for financial stocks, only P/E, P/B and P/D), with a higher percentile 
indicating a stock trading at a higher multiple. The Integrated percentile is calculated as the average of the Growth percentile, Financial Returns 
percentile and (100% - Multiple percentile). 
Financial Returns and Multiple use the Goldman Sachs analyst forecasts at the fiscal year-end at least three quarters in the future. Growth uses inputs 
for the fiscal year at least seven quarters in the future compared with the year at least three quarters in the future (on a per-share basis for all metrics). 
For a more detailed description of how we calculate the GS Factor Profile, please contact your GS representative.  
M&A Rank 
Across our global coverage, we examine stocks using an M&A framework, considering both qualitative factors and quantitative factors (which may vary 
across sectors and regions) to incorporate the potential that certain companies could be acquired. We then assign a M&A rank as a means of scoring 
companies under our rated coverage from 1 to 3, with 1 representing high (30%-50%) probability of the company becoming an acquisition target, 2 
representing medium (15%-30%) probability and 3 representing low (0%-15%) probability. For companies ranked 1 or 2, in line with our standard 
departmental guidelines we incorporate an M&A component into our target price. M&A rank of 3 is considered immaterial and therefore does not 
factor into our price target, and may or may not be discussed in research. 
Quantum 
Quantum is Goldman Sachs’ proprietary database providing access to detailed financial statement histories, forecasts and ratios. It can be used for 
in-depth analysis of a single company, or to make comparisons between companies in different sectors and markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions 
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell 
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an 
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is 
deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists 
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across 
30 September 2026
  63
Goldman Sachs
Americas Insurance
 
 

their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or 
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.    
Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or 
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total 
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.  
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at 
 
.    
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when 
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or 
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment 
rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that 
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs 
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an 
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied 
upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this 
company.

General disclosures

consider reliable, but we do not represent it is accurate or complete, and it should not be relied on as such. The information, opinions, estimates and 
forecasts contained herein are as of the date hereof and are subject to change without prior notification. We seek to update our research as 
appropriate, but various regulations may prevent us from doing so. Other than certain industry reports published on a periodic basis, the large majority 
of reports are published at irregular intervals as appropriate in the analyst’s judgment.

banking and other business relationships with a substantial percentage of the companies covered by Global Investment Research. Goldman Sachs & Co.

discuss in this report, trading strategies that reference catalysts or events that may have a near-term impact on the market price of the equity securities 
discussed in this report, which impact may be directionally counter to the analyst’s published price target expectations for such stocks. Any such 
trading strategies are distinct from and do not affect the analyst’s fundamental equity rating for such stocks, which rating reflects a stock’s return 
potential relative to its coverage universe as described herein.

instruments issued by the companies referred to herein), unless otherwise prohibited by regulation or Goldman Sachs policy.
